# `mantdoc` native parser migration plan

Status: **active — M0–M9 complete; M10/M11 await external soak evidence and separately approved C-source cleanup**
Plan ID: `mantdoc-native-rewrite-v1`
Last reviewed: 2026-08-27
Implementation baseline: `863d2b3867dfb38b7b4db265073a3d4b9b1a10e9`
(`origin/dev` at pull time)
Working branch: `codex/mantdoc-native-rewrite`
Target: replace every runtime, build-time, test, and maintenance dependency on
`libmandoc-rs` with a safe, native Rust crate named `mantdoc`.

This document is the durable source of truth for the migration. It records the
target contract, implementation order, evidence required at each gate, and the
definition of completion. A long-running implementation task should update the
status table and decision log here instead of relying on chat history.

Maintainer approval was recorded on 2026-08-24 and starts milestone M0. This
document remains the durable source of truth for the long-running goal.

Post-compatibility maintainability and lint-debt work is tracked separately in
[`mantdoc-refactoring.md`](mantdoc-refactoring.md), so structural cleanup does
not obscure the migration evidence below.

## 1. Mission and completion boundary

`mantdoc` will be a safe, byte-oriented Rust implementation of the subset of
roff, man(7), mdoc(7), tbl(7), and eqn(7) needed to match and ultimately improve
the current patched libmandoc behavior. Its public interface may be redesigned;
its semantic coverage, owned syntax information, diagnostics, bounded input
handling, and cross-platform behavior may not regress silently.

"Complete replacement" has four separate gates. The migration is complete only
when all four are closed:

1. **Product gate:** `mant-engine` uses `mantdoc` for every native manual on
   Linux, macOS, and Windows; no shipped ManT binary links or builds C code.
2. **Capability gate:** every supported `libmandoc-rs` use case has a documented
   `mantdoc` equivalent: bytes, files, virtual bundles, macro selection,
   caller-controlled includes, compression conveniences, OS metadata override,
   owned AST, diagnostics, optional Serde, and bounded ASCII/UTF-8/HTML reference
   rendering.
3. **Evidence gate:** parser AST, `mant-ir`, renderer, malformed-input, real
   corpus, performance, and cross-platform gates below have no unexplained
   failures.
4. **Repository gate:** `libmandoc-rs`, vendored mandoc C, its build script,
   shims, native dependency installation, symbol/sanitizer maintenance, package
   entries, and obsolete notices are removed. Historical provenance and accepted
   test evidence remain documented.

Passing the product gate is an important release milestone, but is not grounds
to mark the long-running goal complete while any of the other gates remain open.

## 2. Invariants and non-goals

### Invariants

- `mant-ir` remains the source-neutral semantic center. Parser-specific nodes
  never leak into `mant-ir`, `mant-protocol`, the UI, or MCP contracts.
- Core parsing is in-memory and performs no implicit filesystem, environment,
  locale, clock, process, or network access.
- Caller-supplied bytes remain valid for arbitrary non-UTF-8 input. Decoding is
  explicit and recoverable; input is never truncated at NUL or silently replaced
  before syntax decisions are made.
- Every externally influenced loop, recursion, expansion, allocation, include,
  table, equation, and diagnostic path is bounded by one `Limits` value.
- Parser state belongs to one parse session. There are no mutable globals or
  thread-local semantic state, and independent calls are `Send`/`Sync` safe.
- The crate contains no authored `unsafe` code and inherits the workspace's
  `unsafe_code = "deny"`, Rust 1.88 MSRV, edition 2024, and Apache-2.0 policy.
- Best-effort recovery returns useful structure plus diagnostics. Fatal errors
  are reserved for failures that prevent a coherent bounded result.
- Output is deterministic after the caller supplies OS/date/environment values.
- Source I/O, decompression, and `.so` containment remain Rust-owned and do not
  invoke `man`, `mandoc`, `groff`, a shell, or another host process.

### Non-goals

- Implementing a complete device-level troff typesetter, pagination engine,
  terminal pager, man database, or groff extension universe.
- Preserving private libmandoc C layout, pointer lifetimes, enum values, or the
  exact current `libmandoc-rs` Rust type layout.
- Treating byte-identical terminal wrapping, margins, headers, or typography as
  semantic correctness unless the reference-renderer compatibility contract
  explicitly requires it.
- Copying known libmandoc bugs merely to obtain a green diff. Deliberate fixes
  require a narrow, source-hash-keyed accepted-difference record and a regression
  test for the intended behavior.
- Making upstream mandoc HEAD the only oracle. It is an evolving behavior
  reference, not a Rust architecture and not a substitute for the patched local
  baseline.

## 3. Current baseline to preserve

The selected implementation baseline is commit
`863d2b3867dfb38b7b4db265073a3d4b9b1a10e9`. It was fast-forwarded from
`961e967703441f118fda7fe4e440c28207340b28`; the intervening six commits are
part of the compatibility baseline, not pending work for `mantdoc` to ignore.
The migration starts from these checked-in boundaries:

- The workspace changelog and all seven crates are frozen at the 0.9.1 release
  baseline; no compatibility changes are recorded as pending.
- `libmandoc-rs` 0.9.1 exposes owned `Document`, recursive `Node`, normalized
  list/display/font/author/enclosure/table/equation state, metadata, diagnostics,
  `parse_file`, `parse_bytes`, `parse_bundle`, explicit include and compression
  policies, an mdoc OS override, optional Serde, and optional bounded
  ASCII/UTF-8/HTML reference renderers.
- It vendors mandoc 1.14.6 plus 28 local patches covering memory input, modern
  macros and standards, ownership transfer, concurrency, expansion limits,
  deterministic behavior, Windows/MSVC, include policy, and other ManT needs.
- `mant-engine` alone lowers this parser AST into `mant-ir`; it also owns source
  loading, bounded decompression, redirect-only `.so` resolution, terminal-byte
  masking, source-aware recovery, inline eqn normalization, and the user-visible
  renderer pipeline.
- AST text **and validated tags** now normalize libmandoc's private layout
  sentinels. The FFI tests also compare Rust snapshot-struct sizes with the C
  shim, making ABI layout drift an explicit property of the old boundary.
- `libmandoc-rs::DiagnosticCode` now classifies the two wrapper-generated
  truncation findings as `SyntaxTreeDepthLimit` and `EquationTreeDepthLimit`.
  To preserve the 0.9.1 patch-compatible Serde shape, `Diagnostic::code()`
  currently derives those values from exact wrapper messages instead of adding
  a serialized field. `mant-engine` projects them to
  `manual.syntax-depth-truncated` and `manual.equation-depth-truncated`.
- Native syntax-tree and equation copying stop after 256 levels, retain the
  finite prefix, and report the corresponding typed finding. This observable
  success-with-truncation behavior is part of the migration baseline.
- Rust gzip decoding is a Windows production dependency and a cross-platform
  development dependency; Unix production file input still uses libmandoc's
  native zlib transport. Rust zstd decoding remains cross-platform. This
  platform split belongs to the old convenience API, not the proposed parser
  core.
- The repository contains 35 redistributable roff source fixtures (31
  third-party pages and four ManT audit fixtures), focused parser fixtures,
  fidelity/structure/projection/layout ledgers, a `roff_pipeline` fuzz target,
  an AST-transfer benchmark, packaged-crate checks, and native Linux/macOS/
  Windows CI.

M0 must produce a machine-readable capability matrix from the actual public API
and every repository consumer. This section is a summary, not a substitute for
that inventory.

The new baseline changes the plan in five concrete ways:

1. `mantdoc` diagnostics store a real typed code independently of message text.
   The differential adapter calls the old `Diagnostic::code()` accessor rather
   than comparing its patch-compatible serialized representation.
2. The two truncation codes, finite-prefix behavior, and engine projections are
   explicit focused regressions and capability-matrix rows.
3. Text and tag normalization share one tested normalization contract.
4. Native ABI-size tests remain oracle maintenance only and disappear at M11;
   they do not justify exposing `mantdoc` storage layout.
5. `mantdoc` transport adapters remove the old Unix/Windows gzip asymmetry by
   making gzip and zstd explicit, cross-platform Rust features. `mant-engine`
   keeps ownership of its existing decompression path.

## 4. Recommended public contract

The public API should expose behavior, not storage layout. Names below are the
recommended initial design; M1 may refine them before the 0.1.0 API is frozen.

```rust
pub struct Parser {
    config: ParserConfig,
}

pub struct ParserConfig {
    pub syntax: Syntax,
    pub operating_system: Option<Box<str>>,
    pub limits: Limits,
    pub recovery: RecoveryMode,
}

pub enum Syntax {
    Auto,
    Roff,
    Man,
    Mdoc,
}

pub struct Source<'a> {
    pub name: &'a SourceName,
    pub bytes: &'a [u8],
}

impl Parser {
    pub fn parse(&self, source: Source<'_>) -> Result<ParseReport, FatalError>;

    pub fn parse_with_resolver<R: SourceResolver>(
        &self,
        source: Source<'_>,
        resolver: &mut R,
    ) -> Result<ParseReport, FatalError>;
}

pub struct ParseReport {
    pub document: Document,
    pub diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    pub statistics: ParseStatistics,
}
```

The exact signature should observe these rules:

- `parse` is byte-first, allocation-bounded, deterministic, and has includes
  denied. It is the only API required by `mant-engine`.
- A `SourceResolver` returns caller-owned logical sources. It cannot grant
  hidden filesystem access and receives the including source, raw target, and
  remaining limits. `SourceBundle` and contained-root filesystem adapters are
  implementations of this interface, not alternate parser engines.
- File and gzip/zstd conveniences live in an `io` module behind additive
  features. The parser core never guesses transport from a path.
- `FatalError` identifies invalid configuration, exhausted hard resource limits,
  unreadable convenience-I/O sources, decompression failure, and an internal
  invariant violation. Ordinary syntax errors belong in `Diagnostic`.
- Diagnostics store a stable code, severity, primary span, optional related
  spans, and human message. A code is not inferred from its message. Tests
  compare codes and spans; wording may improve without becoming a compatibility
  trap. The optional logical Serde form may expose this first-class code even
  though the redesigned schema is not byte-compatible with the old 0.9.1
  diagnostic representation; the migration guide must call out that change.
- `ParseStatistics` reports bounded counters such as source bytes, source files,
  expansion steps, emitted nodes, maximum depth, and truncation. It supports
  debugging and performance evidence without exposing implementation state.

### Feature policy

The recommended feature surface is:

| Feature | Default | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| parser core | yes | bytes, AST, diagnostics, limits, resolver trait |
| `serde` | no | stable logical serialization, independent of arena layout |
| `gzip` | no | cross-platform Rust `flate2` convenience transport |
| `zstd` | no | cross-platform Rust `zstd` convenience transport |
| `render` | no | bounded ASCII, locale-independent UTF-8, and HTML references |

The exact feature names can be shortened before publication. They must be
additive, testable individually, and must not make ManT production depend on
reference rendering or compression it already owns.

### Compatibility mapping

M0 creates a table with one row for every `libmandoc-rs` public item and every
internal consumer, classified as:

- direct `mantdoc` replacement;
- redesigned replacement with migration example;
- moved to an optional adapter/module;
- intentionally retired with written rationale and maintainer approval.

No public capability may disappear merely because ManT does not currently call
it. The final migration guide must cover source and behavioral differences; the
new crate is not required to be source-compatible.

## 5. Owned syntax tree design

The final AST should retain the semantic information already proven useful by
`libmandoc-rs`, while removing recursive allocation and public storage lock-in.

### Storage

- `Document` owns a contiguous `Vec<NodeRecord>`, a compact child-edge buffer,
  source records, and an internal string table.
- `NodeId` is a checked opaque index. Public traversal uses `Document::root()`,
  `Document::node(NodeId)`, `NodeRef`, child iterators, and iterative preorder/
  ancestor iterators.
- `NodeRecord` and string-table identifiers stay private. Serde emits a logical
  tree/schema, never arena indices or string interning details.
- Parser construction may use mutable builders, but the returned `Document` is
  immutable and owns every byte it exposes. No self-references or bump-arena
  lifetimes enter the public contract.
- Parser and downstream traversal avoid recursive calls over untrusted depth.
  Explicit stacks and `Limits::max_tree_depth` enforce the boundary.

This layout is the frozen pre-0.1 decision because it reduces per-node
allocations, supports cheap IDs and indexing, and lets storage evolve behind
getters. The M1 microbenchmark is recorded in the baseline manifest; a later
change requires evidence and a decision-log entry.

### Logical node content

At minimum the logical AST retains:

- root/block/head/body/tail/element/text/comment/table/equation roles;
- typed known man, mdoc, and roff macro/request identity plus lossless custom or
  unknown names;
- normalized visible text and the source span(s) that produced it;
- normalized validated tags using the same private-sentinel policy as visible
  text, without conflating a tag with display text;
- generated/no-print/sentence/no-fill/link/permalink/line-start/delimiter/
  continuation/synopsis flags currently consumed by lowering;
- list kind, compactness, offset, width, display kind, font, author mode, and
  resolved enclosure state;
- table cell text-block status, alignment, row/column spans, and continuations;
- display and inline eqn normalized form plus source provenance;
- validated tags and metadata: title, manual section, volume, OS, architecture,
  name, date, alias target, and body presence.

Known macro identity should be typed internally and exposed without forcing
callers to allocate strings. Unknown requests and user-defined macros remain
representable; an enum must never turn forward-compatible input into data loss.

### Source model

- A `SourceId` identifies every root or resolved include.
- `Span` uses byte offsets plus `SourceId`; line/column is derived through a
  bounded document-owned line index and exposed as a convenience.
- Expanded/generated nodes can carry an origin chain or related spans so a
  diagnostic can point to both invocation and definition.
- Text decoding is a distinct pass. The scanner operates on bytes and supports
  the existing UTF-8/Latin-1 preservation policy without using lossy UTF-8 to
  make syntax decisions.

## 6. Parser architecture

The implementation is a pipeline of testable modules. Each stage accepts and
returns owned or session-borrowed Rust data; no stage reaches into
`mant-engine`.

```text
bytes + SourceId
    -> source/encoding + line index
    -> roff scanner and argument lexer
    -> escape/copy-mode/numeric evaluator
    -> roff execution and expansion environment
    -> tbl and eqn preprocessors
    -> man or mdoc structural parser
    -> validation, normalization, metadata, and tags
    -> immutable arena Document + diagnostics + statistics
```

### Module responsibilities

1. `source`: byte ownership, encoding decision, logical names, spans, and line
   indexing.
2. `scan`: control lines, text lines, comments, arguments, quoted forms,
   continuations, dynamic control characters (`cc`/`c2`), and dynamic escape
   state (`ec`/`eo`). It must not use `str::lines()` plus trimming as a roff
   grammar.
3. `escape`: left-to-right escape recognition, copy mode, special characters,
   strings/registers, numeric/scaled expressions, delimiters, and visible-text
   normalization. The post-1.14.6 upstream `roff_escape.c` split is a behavior
   reference, not code structure that must be copied.
4. `roff`: per-session strings, number registers, macro definitions, argument
   frames, aliases, translations, conditionals, loops, control flow, and safe
   include requests. All expansion consumes centralized work/output budgets.
5. `tbl`: options, layouts, data rows, text blocks, cell alignment/spans, and
   numeric expressions, reusing the same numeric evaluator as roff.
6. `eqn`: tokens, definitions, delimiters, display/inline forms, and normalized
   expressions with a recursive-definition work budget.
7. `man` and `mdoc`: macro-specific block/element state machines, scope recovery,
   punctuation and spacing roles, synopsis behavior, and semantic validation.
8. `validate`: metadata, tags, normalized attributes, generated nodes,
   cross-node invariants, and final diagnostic classification.
9. `ast`: immutable logical API, traversal, optional Serde, and a canonical test
   projection.
10. `render`: optional bounded reference renderers, deliberately separate from
    the parser and from `mant-engine`'s user-facing renderers.

### Parsing technique

Use a hand-written scanner and state machines for the core. Roff has mutable
control characters, multi-phase escape expansion, copy mode, user macros, and
recovery rules that do not fit a single declarative grammar cleanly. Parser
combinators may be evaluated for isolated pure subgrammars such as numeric
expressions, but they are admitted only after an allocation and throughput
benchmark against the hand-written version.

## 7. Rust dependency policy

Start with the standard library and add small crates only where they buy a
measurable property:

| Crate | Decision | Intended use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `memchr` | recommended | fast newline, control-byte, and escape scanning |
| `bstr` | recommended | byte strings and non-UTF-8-safe inspection |
| `bitflags` | recommended | compact internal/public node and parser flags |
| `smallvec` | measure first | short argument lists, escape components, parser stacks |
| `indexmap` | measure/semantics first | only where insertion order is language-visible |
| `winnow` | local experiment only | isolated numeric/argument grammars if it wins |
| `serde` | optional | logical AST/report serialization |
| `flate2`, `zstd` | optional transport | capability-compatible file/byte conveniences |
| `unicode-width` | render only | reference renderer width calculations |
| `libfuzzer-sys` | dev workspace only | existing fuzz infrastructure |

Do not adopt `nom`, `pest`, `logos`, a regex engine, an arena with self-referential
lifetimes, or a general interning crate by default. Any addition needs:

1. a precise requirement the standard library implementation does not meet;
2. MSRV, license, advisory, and target review through existing supply-chain CI;
3. evidence for hot-path dependencies;
4. no leakage of the dependency's representation into the stable API.

The `mantdoc` crate should have no build script, no `links` key, and no target-
specific parser implementation.

## 8. Reference implementations and test-source intake

### Local patched libmandoc

Until repository cutover, `libmandoc-rs` 0.9.1 from implementation baseline
`863d2b3867dfb38b7b4db265073a3d4b9b1a10e9`, with its exact vendored source and
patch series, is the primary compatibility oracle. M0 records a reproducible
oracle identity containing the workspace commit, crate version, upstream
version, vendor checksum, patch checksums, target, and relevant configuration.
It is not enough to say "mandoc 1.14.6" because the local behavior is materially
patched.

The oracle adapter must retrieve wrapper codes through `Diagnostic::code()`;
plain Serde snapshots intentionally omit that derived value in 0.9.1. Separate
legacy-shape tests preserve the old serialized contract while canonical
diagnostic comparisons operate on the logical code. The adapter must also
exercise normalized validated tags and both 256-level finite-prefix findings.

Before deleting the C implementation, generate canonical AST, diagnostic,
`mant-ir`, and reference-render snapshots for the fixed corpus. Keep their
schema and oracle identity versioned so future maintenance does not require
resurrecting C merely to understand an old decision.

### Upstream stable regressions

The mandoc 1.14.6 `regress/` tree contains 572 inputs (276 mdoc, 107 roff,
99 man, 36 tbl, 32 char, and 22 eqn) and 1,189 expected outputs across ASCII,
Markdown, lint, HTML, tag, and UTF-8 modes. The existing vendor synchronization
intentionally excludes this tree.

M0 freezes this corpus as a separately inventoried, checksum-pinned manifest
after verifying source, license, and provenance. M1's unpublished Rust
conformance harness derives and verifies a deterministic per-file hash inventory
from the archive before any payload is imported. Do not copy the upstream Perl
driver. The harness can select input/output mode, normalize only documented
platform effects, and report a minimal diff. The full corpus belongs to that
unpublished tool so it does not inflate the `mantdoc` crates.io package; focused
package tests remain self-contained.

### Upstream development HEAD

The pinned research reference is OpenBSD source commit
`cf84231e26506943f1ef44249bf2ed248e339483` for relevant regression changes,
with source changes reviewed through `7e4c8f4`. At review time this represents
the development line described upstream as the future mandoc 1.15.1, not a
released stable snapshot.

That regression tree has 618 inputs: 46 more than 1.14.6 and 54 modified common
inputs. It adds useful evidence for left-to-right expansion, nested/delayed
escapes, copy mode, argument delimiters, `.MR`, `.Lb`, `.stringup`/
`.stringdown`, shared tbl numeric parsing, eqn definition budgets, and recovery
fixes.

Treat this lane as behavioral errata and forward-compatibility evidence:

- pin exact commits and hashes; never test against a moving branch;
- keep new and modified HEAD cases distinguishable from stable cases;
- start as non-gating with explicit classifications;
- promote understood cases into the required suite;
- never copy its remaining global/process assumptions into the Rust design.

`mandoc -T tree` is useful for human debugging only. Its documentation permits
format changes, so no test may parse it as a stable machine oracle.

### Issue #13 third-party Rust implementation

The issue linked the `man_parser` crate. As inspected on 2026-08-24, its current
repository is `ljh-sh/roff` at commit
`d5a4154b277673f8713f5aa0ca789bcf1d020cc1`, publishing `roff-cli` 0.2.0 with
library name `roff` under Apache-2.0. The parser is a compact, roughly 1,500-line
`&str` line walker that directly produces JSON and has a small focused test
suite.

Useful ideas to reference independently are its cross-platform/no-native-
dependency stance, nested-list stack, pending `.TP` tag handling, `.so` cycle/
depth guard, and JSON/Markdown/HTML smoke tests. It is not a viable parser core
or correctness oracle because it lacks the roff execution model, byte-safe
input, comprehensive man/mdoc validation, tbl/eqn parsing, source spans,
structured diagnostics, and upstream-scale conformance coverage. Any borrowed
test or code must retain provenance and pass a license/code-quality review;
prefer reimplementing the behavior from specifications and independent tests.

## 9. Testing strategy

Yes, the migration should use per-node differential testing, but not a raw
`Debug` or Serde equality check. Storage, IDs, recovery internals, and deliberate
bug fixes will differ. The required comparison is a versioned **canonical
logical AST** followed by an independent **semantic `mant-ir` comparison**.

### Test layers

| Layer | Evidence | Merge/cutover role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| L0 unit | scanner, escape, numeric, expansion, macro, tbl/eqn, limits | every change |
| L1 focused regressions | minimal licensed sources for each fixed defect | every change |
| L2 AST differential | `libmandoc-rs` vs `mantdoc` canonical nodes | Linux migration CI |
| L3 IR differential | both ASTs lowered to canonical `mant-ir` | primary semantic gate |
| L4 upstream conformance | stable regress inputs/goldens; pinned HEAD delta | stable required, HEAD tracked/promoted |
| L5 product corpora | 35 checked-in roff sources and recorded release corpora | required before cutover |
| L6 robustness | malformed suite, property tests, fuzzing, concurrency | required before cutover |
| L7 nonfunctional | benchmarks, peak memory, MSRV, package, three platforms | required before release |

### Canonical AST differential

The canonical schema includes ordered topology, node kind, macro/request name,
normalized text and tags, comparable source location, all lowering-relevant
flags and attributes, table cells, equations, metadata, and diagnostic
severity/code/location. For the 0.9.1 oracle, the code comes from
`Diagnostic::code()`, not its Serde fields. The schema excludes arena IDs,
string-table IDs, allocation order, internal origin chains unavailable from C,
host-derived default values unless pinned, and diagnostic prose.

Every mismatch receives one classification:

- `mantdoc-bug`;
- `oracle-patch-delta`;
- `upstream-head-correction`;
- `intentional-semantic-fix`;
- `diagnostic-only`;
- `malformed-recovery-difference`;
- `not-yet-implemented` during a milestone only.

Accepted differences live in a checked-in ledger keyed by corpus, decompressed
source SHA-256, canonical field path, oracle identity, reason, and regression
test. No macro-wide or file-pattern-wide ignore is allowed. `not-yet-implemented`
entries block the relevant milestone exit and cannot survive final cutover.

### `mant-ir` differential

During migration, `mant-engine` supports two development-only lowerers/backends:
the current `libmandoc-rs` path and `mantdoc`. For the same bounded source it
canonicalizes and compares:

- parser provenance separately from semantics;
- metadata and source format;
- section/block/inline topology and stable IDs after normalization;
- list/definition/table/equation/layout semantics;
- links, tags, navigation targets, and diagnostics;
- text, Markdown, JSON, CommonMark round-trip, and relevant renderer results.

Unexplained IR differences are release blockers even when the low-level AST
diff is accepted. This prevents matching libmandoc's node shape while losing
the behavior ManT actually exposes.

### Malformed and adversarial input

Exact recovery-tree equality is not the initial target. From M2 onward, every
malformed case must terminate within work/memory limits, avoid panic and stack
overflow, produce deterministic diagnostics, and preserve a finite useful
prefix where possible. Exact stable diagnostic codes and selected recovery
shapes are tightened milestone by milestone.

Fuzzing must split responsibilities:

- `mantdoc_parse`: arbitrary bytes through every syntax selection;
- `mantdoc_differential`: bounded bytes through both parsers while C remains;
- `mantdoc_pipeline`: parse, traverse every node, lower to `mant-ir`, index,
  project, and render;
- targeted escape/numeric/tbl/eqn state-machine fuzz targets;
- bundle/resolver fuzzing with generated logical source graphs, cycles, missing
  files, and depth/byte limits.

Minimized findings become named regression fixtures before fuzz artifacts are
discarded. The existing 64 KiB general fuzz cap remains the starting point;
separate generated tests exercise large valid documents and each exact limit.

### Renderer evidence

Reference renderer work has two standards:

1. semantic output agrees after documented normalization of wrapping, margins,
   headers/footers, and device typography;
2. exact goldens are used where upstream's regression contract is stable and
   environment-independent.

Current fidelity, projection, structure, layout, and reference-deviation
ledgers remain independent. A clean visible-token diff does not excuse wrong
AST/IR topology, and a clean AST diff does not excuse bad output.

## 10. Resource and security model

`Limits` is a public, validated configuration with conservative defaults and at
least these independent counters:

- root and aggregate source bytes;
- resolved source count, physical source lines, include depth, and include target length;
- physical/logical line length;
- expansion steps and expanded output bytes;
- macro call depth, argument count/bytes, and loop iterations;
- string/register/macro definition count and aggregate storage;
- AST nodes, child edges, text bytes, and tree depth;
- tbl rows, columns, cells, spans, and text-block bytes;
- eqn tokens, nesting, definitions, and expansion steps;
- diagnostics and related spans;
- reference renderer output bytes and traversal work.

Budget exhaustion returns a stable diagnostic and bounded partial result when
coherent; it becomes fatal only when the root result cannot be represented.
Wall-clock timeouts are not a substitute for deterministic work counters.

The resolver contract must make path policy explicit. The crate supplies:

- deny-all resolver/default;
- in-memory `SourceBundle` with canonical logical paths and aggregate limits;
- contained-root adapter with platform-correct path handling, symlink policy,
  cycle detection, and no working-directory fallback.

`mant-engine` may continue resolving redirect-only indexed pages itself. The
parser never broadens that product policy.

## 11. Performance plan

M0 captures release-profile baselines on the same host for:

- current generated small/medium/large AST-transfer cases;
- the full fixed fixture set;
- syntax-balanced stable upstream samples;
- representative real small, median, large, tbl-heavy, eqn-heavy, and macro-
  expansion-heavy pages;
- parse-only, parse-plus-lower, and parse-plus-render paths;
- allocations/owned bytes, node count, maximum depth, and peak resident memory;
- parallel independent parses.

The benchmark records toolchain, target, CPU, commit, oracle identity, sample
manifest, and statistical method. The existing dependency-free benchmark is
extended first; a statistical dev dependency is added only if it produces a
more reliable gate.

Provisional final-cutover thresholds, adjustable only from M0 evidence with a
decision-log entry, are:

- mixed-corpus parse throughput is at least the `libmandoc-rs` baseline;
- no representative class is more than 10% slower at the median or 20% slower
  at p95 without an approved correctness trade-off;
- parse-plus-lower is not slower than the current production pipeline;
- peak memory and owned bytes are no more than 125% of baseline on large pages;
- parallel throughput scales without a global parser lock;
- pathological inputs stop by deterministic budgets, not an OS OOM or timeout.

Performance changes never bypass semantic, sanitizer/fuzz, or package tests.

## 12. Milestones and exit gates

Milestones are dependency-ordered, not calendar estimates. Each one ends with a
small reviewable commit series, updated evidence, and this document's status
row. A milestone is not complete because most cases pass.

### M0 — Contract freeze and reproducible baselines

Deliverables:

- machine-readable `libmandoc-rs` capability/consumer matrix;
- exact patched-oracle identity and source/patch checksums;
- initial M0 artifacts under `tests/conformance/manifests/v1/`, including the
  oracle, corpus, capability, baseline, and canonical-differential contracts;
- a frozen old-to-new baseline review for
  `961e967703441f118fda7fe4e440c28207340b28..863d2b3867dfb38b7b4db265073a3d4b9b1a10e9`,
  including tag normalization, FFI ABI-size assertions, typed truncation codes,
  finite-prefix behavior, engine code projection, and gzip dependency scope;
- benchmark baseline and representative sample manifest;
- licensed manifests for mandoc 1.14.6 regressions, pinned HEAD delta, current
  fixtures, and third-party reference cases;
- canonical AST/diagnostic/IR diff schemas and accepted-difference format;
- explicit current numeric resource limits and parser behavior matrix;
- skeleton unpublished conformance harness design.

Exit gate: every existing capability and repository consumer has an owner and a
planned destination; all reference inputs are reproducible and legally
inventoried; no parser implementation decision depends on an unrecorded local
environment.

M0 closed on 2026-08-24. Its versioned manifests enumerate 76 legacy public
items/methods, six repository consumer groups, 17 capabilities, and six corpus
lanes. The verifier rejects API or corpus drift, an incomplete 28-patch oracle,
an Oracle-ID mismatch, and malformed accepted-difference records. Evidence:
`python3 scripts/check-mantdoc-conformance-manifests.py`,
`bash crates/libmandoc-rs/scripts/sync-vendor --verify`,
`cargo bench --locked --package libmandoc-rs --bench ast_transfer`, and the
out-of-sandbox `cargo test --locked --workspace` required by the local test
server fixtures. The stable source archive is checksum-pinned; the development
lane is pinned to commit `cf84231e` and its `regress/usr.bin/mandoc` Git tree.

### M1 — Crate skeleton, contracts, and storage prototype

Deliverables:

- `crates/mantdoc` 0.1.0 in the workspace, parser-only by default;
- source/span/diagnostic/error/limit types and immutable arena AST;
- iterative traversal, logical Serde prototype, rustdoc, package contents, and
  API examples;
- arena-vs-recursive microbenchmark and finalized storage decision;
- conformance package/harness that can run one case through named backends and
  read-only verify the stable upstream case inventory before any later payload
  promotion; each backend run rejects a source or parser-configuration hash
  mismatch before parsing.

Exit gate: empty/minimal documents and fatal configuration paths are fully
bounded; all feature combinations compile on MSRV and current stable; packaged
crate tests do not depend on sibling repository files.

Implementation evidence is ready for review: `mantdoc` and the unpublished
`mantdoc-conformance` crate compile with no default features, all features,
current stable, and Rust 1.88; the package listings contain only their own
crate files; focused tests and Clippy pass.  The M1 arena-versus-recursive
50,000-node completed-tree microbenchmark recorded 9,400,184 versus 10,400,208
bytes and 150,987 versus 168,364 ns per preorder traversal on the M0 host.
The selected design remains contiguous `NodeRecord` plus compact `NodeId` edge
arrays, with transient builder capacity released at `Document` completion.
`Document` now retains a root-source map: AST and diagnostic spans carry opaque
`SourceId`, while public lookup derives logical source names and line/byte-column
positions; logical Serde projects names and positions rather than source-map IDs.
The source map has its own 16,777,216-line aggregate M1 ceiling, so a newline-
dense but byte-bounded source cannot create an unbounded `u32` line-index vector.
The stable archive inventory independently verifies its compressed SHA-256 and
the canonical hash of all 572 input plus 1,189 expected-output records without
copying upstream payload into the repository.
Named backend runs validate the exact decompressed source hash plus a
cross-platform canonical syntax/operating-system/recovery/limits fingerprint
before producing a differential record.
This is the frozen pre-0.1 contract for the native implementation. It remains
unpublished until the completion gates close, but M2 and later milestones build
against it rather than changing it silently.

### M2 — Byte scanner, arguments, escapes, and numeric expressions

Deliverables:

- byte-safe lines/control/comments/arguments/quotes/continuations;
- dynamic control and escape character handling;
- left-to-right escapes, copy-mode primitives, named/special characters,
  strings/register references, and shared numeric/scaled evaluator;
- source maps and stable scanner/escape diagnostics;
- unit, property, fuzz, stable roff/char, and pinned HEAD escape cases.

Exit gate: selected upstream roff/char cases have documented scanner-stage
visible-text, token/span, and diagnostic expectations; arbitrary bytes cannot
panic, recurse without a bound, or allocate outside limits. Full canonical AST
comparison begins at M7, when the legacy AST adapter exists; M2 must not claim
that unavailable comparison early.

### M3 — Roff execution engine

Deliverables:

- macro/string/register definition, append, remove, rename, alias, and arguments;
- conditionals, else chains, loops, breaks/returns/shifts, translations, and
  supported compatibility requests;
- correct expansion phases and copy mode for nested/delayed escapes;
- resolver calls, include graphs, cycles, and aggregate budgets;
- deterministic session environment and bare `.Os` override plumbing.

Exit gate: the hash-bound [`m3-execution.toml`](../../tests/conformance/manifests/v1/m3-execution.toml)
stable roff execution cases and selected patched-local cases pass; all expansion
bombs stop at the documented limit with stable diagnostics; parallel sessions
share no semantic state. The M3 runner uses explicit `Syntax::Roff`, so its
exact source identity, raw-roff node/expansion counts, truncation state, and
diagnostic code/span sequence cannot drift when M4+ add macro-package tree
lowering. It does not claim the M7 AST/renderer differential early.

### M4 — man(7) parser and validator

Deliverables:

- man block/element scopes, headings, paragraphs, lists, fonts, no-fill,
  synopsis macros, modern `.MR`, tags, punctuation, and recovery;
- metadata and generated-node behavior required by lowering;
- canonical AST and IR adapter coverage for man fixtures.

Exit gate: all stable man inputs parse through the checksum-pinned M4 smoke
gate without truncation (every currently emitted malformed/recovery/style
diagnostic sequence is explicit); every man AST/IR mismatch is fixed or narrowly classified;
current real man fixtures and projections have no unexplained regression.

### M5 — mdoc(7) parser and validator

Deliverables:

- mdoc block/element/full/partial-call scopes and delimiter rules;
- lists, displays, functions, references, authors, libraries/standards,
  enclosures, synopsis behavior, tags, metadata, and recovery;
- normalized attributes matching all current lowering requirements.

Exit gate: all stable mdoc inputs parse; every mdoc AST/IR mismatch is fixed or
narrowly classified; current real mdoc fixtures and navigation semantics have
no unexplained regression.

For local full-corpus work, use
`python3 scripts/run-mantdoc-differential-shards.py /tmp/mandoc-1.14.6.tar.gz`.
It builds the feature-gated tools once and schedules the independent M3/M4/M6
whole-corpus gates with M5/L2/M8 case-index partitions concurrently.  The
default uses up to twelve process shards and up to twenty concurrent workers
(both bounded by available CPUs); use `--shards 4 --jobs 4` in CI to avoid
runner oversubscription, or lower `--jobs` on a busy development host.  The aggregate preserves
deterministic source order while avoiding a serial full-oracle wait, and only
failed shards print their detailed output unless `--verbose` is requested.
For parallel M9 triage, append `--lanes m9 --list-renderer-differences`; it
prints each shard's stable local renderer differences without a slow serial
whole-corpus replay.
Release evidence retains the unsharded commands for a final independent replay.

### M6 — tbl(7) and eqn(7)

Deliverables:

- tables, layouts, rows, spans, text blocks, alignments, options, and shared
  numeric expressions;
- display and delimiter-selected inline equations, definitions, normalized
  forms, and recursive-definition budgets;
- removal of `mant-engine`'s need to re-enter libmandoc for inline eqn;
- focused and upstream tbl/eqn differential/fuzz suites.

Exit gate: all stable tbl/eqn inputs and current table/equation fixtures pass
AST, IR, and visible-semantic gates; no opaque source-recovery workaround is
silently lost.

### M7 — Full parser parity and public conveniences

Deliverables:

- file, bundle, contained-root resolver, gzip, and zstd adapters;
- complete stable diagnostic/code mapping and metadata/tag behavior;
- first-class native equivalents of `SyntaxTreeDepthLimit` and
  `EquationTreeDepthLimit`, plus compatibility tests for finite prefixes and
  their 256-level default boundary;
- optional Serde schema tests;
- pinned HEAD delta classified and supported cases promoted;
- public API migration guide from `libmandoc-rs`.

Exit gate: the capability matrix has no unimplemented parser/I/O/Serde row;
full stable upstream input suite and all checked-in parser fixtures pass; HEAD
has no unexplained result.

### M8 — `mant-engine` dual integration and product parity

Deliverables:

- `mantdoc` lowering path using native node traversal;
- development-only backend selection and batch AST/IR differential tools;
- current source recovery, aliases, diagnostics, definitions, links,
  projections, and renderer audits adapted to both backends;
- parser provenance changed deliberately from `libmandoc` to `mantdoc`, with
  protocol/golden updates isolated from semantic changes.
- engine projection of native truncation findings remains exactly
  `manual.syntax-depth-truncated` and `manual.equation-depth-truncated`.

Exit gate: every checked-in page produces no unexplained `mant-ir` or output
difference; all existing audit self-checks and ledgers remain coherent; ManT can
run its complete test suite with `libmandoc-rs` disabled.

### M9 — Native reference renderers

Deliverables:

- bounded ASCII, locale-independent UTF-8, and HTML reference formats behind
  `render`;
- upstream stable output-golden harness and documented normalizations;
- output limits, malformed traversal tests, and package-level API tests;
- migration of fuzz/audit users of `libmandoc-rs::Renderer`.

Exit gate: every renderer capability-matrix row is implemented; stable relevant
goldens and semantic renderer audits pass or have exact accepted differences;
no test or tool needs the C renderer.

### M10 — Robustness, performance, and cross-platform release candidate

Deliverables:

- long fuzz runs and minimized regressions for all native targets;
- concurrency stress, Miri-compatible focused tests, limit-boundary and large-
  valid-document suites;
- release benchmark report against M0;
- Linux x64, macOS arm64, Windows x64 MSVC, MSRV, all-features, no-default-
  features, docs.rs, cargo-deny, notice, and staged-package evidence;
- real-corpus sweeps over the recorded Arch, Debian, Fedora, BSD, and Windows
  release sources using deterministic native structure coverage.

Exit gate: all final thresholds in sections 9–11 pass; every review/hard audit
candidate is adjudicated; no global lock or platform-specific parser path
exists.

### M11 — Default switch, deprecation window, and repository removal

Deliverables:

- switch `mant-engine` and fuzz/audit tooling to `mantdoc` by default;
- at least one release/development soak with `libmandoc-rs` retained only as a
  non-default oracle, followed by maintainer approval to remove it;
- locked canonical snapshots generated before oracle deletion;
- removal of crate, vendor C, shims, `cc`, native dependency installation,
  C-specific ABI-size/sanitizer/symbol scripts, package/release entries, and
  stale docs;
- license/notices regenerated from the new Rust-only locked graph;
- `mantdoc` publication, changelog, migration notes, and final architecture
  diagrams.

Exit gate: all four completion gates in section 1 are closed from a clean clone;
`rg` finds no active code/config/documentation that claims libmandoc is the
current parser; packaged artifacts contain no mandoc C object or obsolete
license payload.

## 13. CI evolution

Add checks incrementally so incomplete milestones do not weaken existing gates:

1. M1–M3: `mantdoc` unit/package/MSRV/all-feature jobs plus fuzz compilation;
   current product remains on `libmandoc-rs`.
2. M4–M7: Linux runs selected then full stable AST differential; pure Rust
   conformance runs on macOS and Windows without the C oracle.
3. M8–M9: full AST/IR/output differential on Linux; all three platforms run the
   full fixed native corpus through `mantdoc`.
4. M10: scheduled/explicit long fuzz and broad real-corpus reports; deterministic
   focused regressions remain per-push gates.
5. M11: remove C compiler/zlib/native-symbol/FFI sanitizer jobs only after their
   replacement evidence is green. Retain ordinary thread stress, fuzzing, Miri,
   and package checks.

The full upstream corpus and oracle dependencies live in an unpublished
conformance package. The published `mantdoc` package contains enough focused
tests and fixtures to prove every public feature from Cargo's staged source.

## 14. Risk register

| Risk | Mitigation and evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Roff expansion is larger than expected | implement scanner/execution before macro packages; import stable tests; central work budgets |
| Overfitting to local 1.14.6 bugs | use `mant-ir`, independent renderer audits, pinned HEAD, specifications, and accepted-difference review |
| A prettier AST loses lowering data | capability matrix plus canonical node and independent IR diff |
| Arena design harms ergonomics | opaque getters/iterators, examples, M1 benchmark, logical Serde independent of storage |
| Invalid bytes change syntax | byte-first scanner, spans over original sources, dedicated encoding tests |
| Recovery loops or memory bombs | deterministic counters, explicit stacks, fuzzing, exact limit tests |
| Includes escape approved roots | deny by default; resolver abstraction; contained-root and graph tests on Windows/Unix |
| Parser is fast in isolation but slows ManT | gate parse-plus-lower and full pipeline, not only parse microbenchmarks |
| Renderer parity delays product switch | product and repository gates are separate; C renderer can remain oracle-only until M9, but final completion still requires replacement |
| Upstream changes during the rewrite | immutable stable/HEAD manifests; review newer snapshots only at milestone boundaries |
| Test corpus creates license/package bloat | provenance manifest, unpublished conformance package, focused packaged tests |
| Long task loses context | this file, status table, decision log, exact evidence artifacts, and one active integration wave at a time |

## 15. Long-running execution protocol

After maintainer approval and creation of a long-running goal:

1. Keep one integration wave active at a time. Work may fan out only along the
   dependency graph and ownership rules in section 16; a later wave cannot
   merge or claim completion while an earlier join gate is open.
2. Break the milestone into reviewable vertical slices; every parser feature
   lands with unit, differential/conformance, malformed/limit, and where
   applicable IR tests.
3. Preserve unrelated worktree changes. Never hide failures with broad
   allowlists or regenerate goldens without reviewing the semantic diff.
4. At each checkpoint update the status table, evidence links/commands, accepted
   differences, risk register, and decision log in the same change.
5. A failure in a later layer is debugged at the earliest differing layer:
   scanner -> expansion -> AST -> IR -> renderer. Do not patch a renderer to
   conceal a parser defect.
6. Use exact source hashes and versioned schemas for every persisted comparison.
7. Do not silently move the implementation baseline with `origin/dev`. A future
   rebase/pull records the old and new commits, reviews the bounded diff, and
   updates sections 3, 8, 12, and 19 before implementation resumes.
8. Stop for maintainer input only when a choice changes the public contract,
   completion definition, security policy, license boundary, or an approved
   performance/correctness threshold. Ordinary implementation choices remain
   autonomous within this plan.
9. Mark the goal complete only after M11 and the four section-1 gates pass from
   a clean clone.

## 16. Parallel and sub-agent execution model

Parallelism is used to shorten independent evidence and implementation paths,
not to let multiple writers redesign the same parser state machine. Creating a
long-running goal does not implicitly enable sub-agents: the maintainer must
explicitly authorize this execution model when starting or resuming that goal.
No sub-agent is started merely because this section exists.

### Integration waves and dependency graph

One integration wave is active at the program level. A wave may contain
multiple bounded child targets whose dependencies are already frozen:

```text
W0: M0
 -> W1: M1
 -> W2: M2
 -> W3: M3
 -> W4: M4 man | M5 mdoc | M6 tbl+eqn
 -> W5: M7 parity
 -> W6: M8 engine integration | M9 reference renderers
 -> W7: M10 release-candidate evidence
 -> W8: M11 switch, soak, removal, release
```

M4, M5, and M6 may run concurrently only after M3 and the shared AST builder,
diagnostic, limit, numeric, and parser-session interfaces are frozen. M8 and M9
may run concurrently only after M7 freezes the logical AST and public parser
contract. A child target can finish early, but its parent wave remains open
until the coordinator runs the combined join gate.

The default ceiling is one coordinator plus at most three sub-agents. Use fewer
when fewer non-overlapping targets exist. Worker count is never itself a
progress metric.

### Coordinator-owned decisions and files

The primary goal/coordinator remains the single owner of:

- this plan, the active-wave status, the decision log, and completion claims;
- public API and feature decisions;
- shared AST records/builders, parser session/environment, diagnostics and
  `Limits` contracts, canonical schemas, and accepted-difference policy;
- root workspace membership/dependency policy and `Cargo.lock` integration;
- final review of generated goldens, audit-ledger conclusions, licenses,
  security boundaries, and performance threshold changes;
- baseline pulls/rebases, branch changes, releases, and removal of the old
  oracle.

Sub-agents do not pull, rebase, switch branches, reset files, regenerate broad
goldens, edit accepted-difference ledgers, or change public contracts unless an
individual task packet explicitly grants that exact action. Because agents
share one worktree, exclusive path ownership is mandatory even when Git would
eventually be able to merge the edits.

### Eligible parallel child targets

The stable child-target IDs below make delegation resumable and auditable. The
coordinator may split a target more narrowly, but must not combine targets in a
way that defeats their file ownership.

| Wave | Child target | Scope | Join dependency |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| W0/M0 | `m0_api_inventory` | public API, feature, consumer, and repository-removal matrix | coordinator approves capability classification |
| W0/M0 | `m0_corpus_provenance` | stable/HEAD/real/third-party manifests, checksums, licenses | provenance audit passes |
| W0/M0 | `m0_benchmark_baseline` | sample manifest, benchmark commands, machine/toolchain identity, results | coordinator approves thresholds |
| W0/M0 | `m0_diff_contract` | canonical AST/diagnostic/IR schema and narrow difference-ledger format | coordinator freezes schema |
| W1/M1 | `m1_conformance_harness` | unpublished harness/package using the coordinator-frozen API | staged package and one-backend smoke gate |
| W1/M1 | `m1_package_docs` | rustdoc, examples, feature/package checks | public examples reviewed against the frozen contract |
| W2/M2 | `m2_roff_char_cases` | upstream case manifesting and focused scanner/escape tests | core scanner passes selected cases |
| W2/M2 | `m2_fuzz_harness` | scanner/escape/numeric fuzz targets and seed curation | no changes to parser semantics |
| W3/M3 | `m3_resolver_tests` | bundle/include graph, cycle, containment, and limit fixtures | execution engine resolver contract passes |
| W3/M3 | `m3_expansion_cases` | upstream/local macro, copy-mode, conditional, and bomb cases | core execution engine passes cases |
| W4/M4 | `m4_man_parser` | `man` module, its fixtures, canonical AST adapter cases | shared interfaces frozen; full man exit gate |
| W4/M5 | `m5_mdoc_parser` | `mdoc` module, its fixtures, canonical AST adapter cases | shared interfaces frozen; full mdoc exit gate |
| W4/M6 | `m6_tbl_eqn` | `tbl`/`eqn` modules, fixtures, normalization and fuzz targets | shared numeric/AST interfaces frozen; M6 gate |
| W5/M7 | `m7_io_adapters` | file, bundle, contained-root, gzip, and zstd conveniences | capability matrix and cross-platform tests |
| W5/M7 | `m7_serde_migration` | logical Serde schema, legacy comparison, migration examples | public schema review |
| W5/M7 | `m7_head_triage` | pinned HEAD execution and candidate classification report | coordinator approves every promotion/difference |
| W6/M8 | `m8_engine_lowering` | `mant-engine` adapter/lowering and provenance migration | complete AST/IR differential |
| W6/M8 | `m8_audit_tooling` | batch differential profiles and existing audit integration | audit self-checks and fixed-corpus replay |
| W6/M9 | `m9_text_renderers` | bounded ASCII and UTF-8 reference rendering | shared render traversal/output policy frozen |
| W6/M9 | `m9_html_renderer` | bounded HTML reference rendering | shared render traversal/output policy frozen |
| W6/M9 | `m9_golden_harness` | upstream output selection/normalization and package tests | coordinator reviews exact and normalized diffs |
| W7/M10 | `m10_fuzz_robustness` | long fuzzing, Miri-focused tests, concurrency and limit reports | minimized findings committed as regressions |
| W7/M10 | `m10_performance` | release benchmark and memory report against M0 | final thresholds pass |
| W7/M10 | `m10_platform_ci` | Linux/macOS/Windows/MSRV/features/package evidence | all required jobs pass exact commit |
| W7/M10 | `m10_real_corpora` | recorded distribution/release sweeps and syntax coverage | every candidate adjudicated by coordinator |
| W8/M11 | `m11_build_cleanup` | C/native dependency, scripts, package, CI, and artifact cleanup | clean-clone Rust-only verification |
| W8/M11 | `m11_docs_licenses` | architecture/manual/changelog/migration/license/notice updates | coordinator verifies code and documents agree |

M1's `mantdoc` public contract/storage prototype, M2's scanner and escape
semantics, M3's roff execution environment, M7's final parity decisions, and
M11's default switch/removal remain coordinator-led single-writer work. Their
supporting tests and evidence may be delegated as shown.

### Required task packet

Every child target receives a self-contained task packet before it starts:

1. stable child-target ID and one-sentence objective;
2. exact baseline commit and required completed dependency/gate;
3. exclusive allowed paths and explicit must-not-touch paths;
4. input manifests/specifications and expected output artifact;
5. focused verification commands and success criteria;
6. stop/escalation conditions, including public-contract ambiguity, license
   uncertainty, an unexpected semantic oracle difference, or a shared-file
   conflict;
7. required handoff: changed paths, tests/results, unresolved findings, and any
   assumptions made.

A task is not delegable if its output cannot be reviewed independently, if it
requires simultaneous edits to coordinator-owned core files, or if the only
success criterion is "make the suite green".

### Join and conflict protocol

For each child result, the coordinator:

1. confirms the baseline/dependency snapshot still matches;
2. reviews the complete diff and rejects out-of-scope changes;
3. runs the child's focused verification rather than trusting a prose report;
4. resolves shared integration edits itself;
5. runs the combined wave differential/conformance/limit gate;
6. updates the status/evidence and only then closes the child target.

Sub-agent completion is provisional. It never marks a milestone, wave, product
gate, or long-running goal complete. A conflict in shared files pauses the later
writer; agents must not solve it by overwriting, resetting, broad allowlisting,
or independently regenerating snapshots. Unexpected semantic differences return
to the coordinator for classification.

### Status tracking

At most one integration wave is `in_progress`. Within it, child targets use
`pending`, `in_progress`, `review`, or `completed` in the milestone evidence
artifact. M4–M6 and M8–M9 are the only milestone groups that may simultaneously
show `in_progress`, and only inside W4 and W6 respectively. The main status table
below continues to report exit-gate completion, not worker activity.

## 18. Recommended decisions for approval

Approving this plan means accepting these defaults unless the maintainer lists
an amendment:

1. `mantdoc` may redesign its API; capability and semantic parity are required,
   source compatibility is not.
2. The final owned AST uses opaque `NodeId` plus contiguous arena/edge/string
   storage and logical getters/Serde, subject to the M1 evidence checkpoint.
3. The core is a hand-written byte scanner and state machines; parsing crates
   are limited to benchmarked local subgrammars.
4. Parser-only is the default build; Serde, compression transports, and
   reference rendering are additive features.
5. Patched local 1.14.6 is the migration compatibility oracle, `mant-ir` is the
   semantic product oracle, stable upstream regressions are the conformance
   base, and pinned development HEAD is an errata/forward lane.
6. Per-node differential testing is mandatory but canonicalized; exact internal
   storage and malformed recovery are not blindly equated.
7. Product cutover may precede deletion of the C oracle, but the goal remains
   incomplete until renderer/tooling parity and repository removal are done.
8. The provisional performance and memory thresholds in section 11 apply unless
   M0 data justifies a reviewed amendment.
9. Parallel work follows the wave/task-packet/single-writer/join model in
   section 16; sub-agents are used only after explicit maintainer authorization
   for the long-running goal.

Indirect requests resolve both target and terminator through the session string table before copy mode. Focused direct and indirect append regressions pass; stable `roff/de/startde` is diagnostic-clean and untruncated. | active |
| 2026-08-25 | close conditional scopes after appended control closers | Scope collection now recognizes a closing `\\}` attached to an ordinary request, including the stable `.br\\}` form. It retains the request without its closer before ending the current explicit frame, so a true `.ie` body cannot swallow its following `.el` branch and expose `\\{\\` as malformed text. A focused regression and checksum-verified `roff/cond/ie` are diagnostic-clean and untruncated. | active |
| 2026-08-25 | execute braced conditionals in every macro copy frame | Both deferred macro paths—the top-level macro executor and macros invoked from collected scopes—now consume a selected `\\{…\\}` range only from their current invocation depth, then replay it iteratively with the original argument frame. They track immediate `.ie/.el` state per macro depth, so inactive bodies cannot leak and neither recursion nor a caller's next request is consumed. Focused two-branch coverage, including scope-macro execution, and checksum-verified `roff/de/factorial` are diagnostic-clean and untruncated. Nonstandard same-line delimiters and richer nested else chains remain M3 work. | active |
| 2026-08-25 | complete nested conditional scope delimiter collection | Scope collection now distinguishes inline `\\{…\\}` delimiters from an opener that requires later physical input, preserves same-line initial bodies at every nesting level, and consumes consecutive `\\}` controls on text or request lines without leaking frames. The outermost closer keeps its post-closer text in its physical generated node, preserving loop output such as `\\n[count]\\},`. Focused nested control-closer coverage plus checksum-verified `roff/cond/{if,ie}` are diagnostic-clean and untruncated; `roff/cond/close` reports only its intentional unterminated final scope. | active |
| 2026-08-25 | bind nested `.ie/.el` state to scope execution frames | Collected nested `.ie` and `.el` blocks now have typed logical roles. The iterative executor carries one immediately eligible branch state with each sibling-line frame, selecting only the false `.el` arm and expiring the state on any intervening request. This avoids global parser state and keeps nested branch eligibility isolated. A focused nested `.ie/.el` regression and checksum-verified `roff/cond/{if,ie}` are diagnostic-clean and untruncated. | active |
| 2026-08-25 | prove per-session execution state remains isolated in parallel | A focused multi-threaded parser regression gives each independent parse the same delayed-string name but a different value, then verifies every resulting document retains only its own value. The native session remains entirely stack/heap owned by one parse call; broader concurrency stress remains an M10 release gate. | active |
| 2026-08-25 | execute generated definitions from scope macros | A macro invoked from a collected scope can now execute `.de`, `.am`, `.dei`, and `.ami` against subsequent physical copy-mode input, with the same target/terminator lookup, size bounds, and recovery diagnostics as the top-level macro path. A focused indirect append regression proves no caller frame or source cursor is desynchronized. | active |
| 2026-08-25 | refresh the selected M3 stable-execution evidence | The checksum-verified 1.14.6 runner reports diagnostic-clean, untruncated outcomes for `roff/while/{basic,break,nesting,into,outof}`, `roff/de/{factorial,startde,tab}`, `roff/ds/{append,nested,quoting}`, and `roff/ig/basic`. `roff/de/indir` remains untruncated with exactly its two deliberate undefined-indirect-name findings. | 2026-08-25 | execute copy-mode definitions retained in collected scopes | The scope executor now recognizes retained `.de`, `.am`, `.dei`, and `.ami` bodies before generic control lowering, consumes only their local terminating range, and reconstructs nested retained scopes as deferred copy-mode lines, preserving `.ie` rather than collapsing it to `.if`. | 2026-08-25 | execute inline `.ie/.el` alternatives in collected loops | The explicit scope-line executor now evaluates a non-braced `.ie`, carries its immediate branch result only to the next sibling frame, and dispatches the eligible `.el` body through the normal scope control/text path. | 2026-08-25 | dispatch inline conditional macro bodies inside scopes | A selected inline `.if`/`.ie` body that is a user macro now takes the existing iterative scope-macro execution path rather than becoming visible request text. | 2026-08-25 | isolate collector-time dynamic scanner state | Brace-scope collection needs temporary `.cc`/`.c2`/`.ec` changes to classify later physical lines, but those changes must not take effect merely because a condition was inspected. | 2026-08-25 | execute brace-scoped while bodies inside scope macros | The scope-macro executor now pulls `.while \{…\}` bodies from its bounded pending copy-mode frame rather than the physical scanner, re-evaluates the caller argument environment each iteration, and propagates break/continue without recursion across iterations. | 2026-08-25 | close the M3 roff execution gate | The native executor now has bounded definitions, dynamic names/characters, conditionals and else state, loops in physical/macro/scope-macro frames, copy mode, includes, translation, and session-local state. 121 native unit tests, the 29-case checksum-bound M3 runner, manifest validation, and Linux CI integration pass. | 2026-08-25 | add a bounded contained-root filesystem resolver | `ContainedRootResolver` now canonicalizes its root and each resolved file, maps accepted canonical paths to root-relative POSIX logical names, reads at most the smaller of per-file and remaining graph byte budgets, and rejects absolute, escaping, and symlink-outside requests without a working-directory fallback. `parse_file_in_root` is the explicit root convenience entry point; a missing/rejected later include remains a source-addressable recovery diagnostic. | 2026-08-25 | retain formatter-only and single-byte special escapes in the package AST | mandoc classifies `\\%`, `\\&`, `\\)`, `\\,`, `\\/`, `\\^`, `\\a`, `\\d`, `\\r`, `\\t`, `\\u`, `\\{`, `\\|`, and `\\}` as no-argument ignored escapes. | 2026-08-27 | retain double-box slots and allbox/manual tbl boundaries | A `doublebox` now emits both top and bottom terminal rules and contributes two deferred table slots, so `.sp 2v` immediately after it consumes the required device spacing without an extra blank. `allbox` now adds its own boundary before a later content row even when an authored `_`/`=` rule also occurs there; that automatic rule inherits the authored rule's vertical frame. Focused renderer tests plus `regress/{man/TS/vspace,tbl/opt/box}` are byte-identical. Strict native Clippy passes, and the deterministic twelve-way M9 replay advances to **644 exact / 15 different / 0 errors** across 659 artifacts. | active |
| 2026-08-27 | model tbl `x` field allocation and narrowed line lengths | Renderer-private tbl cells now retain `x` expansion metadata. The terminal width pass mirrors upstream `tblcalc`: it partitions the remaining field with the historical left-biased rounding and the GNU-compatible five-column quirk, but leaves minimum widths intact when fixed fields already exceed the active `.ll` width. Borderless styled tables no longer pretend to have a trailing border slot, so a following `.sp` remains visible. The public `TableCell`/canonical AST are unchanged. Focused parser/renderer coverage plus `regress/tbl/mod/{expand,expand-toowide}` are byte-identical; `cargo test -p mantdoc --all-features`, strict native Clippy, and a concurrent twelve-way M9 replay pass at **645 exact / 14 different / 0 errors** across 659 artifacts. | active |
| 2026-08-27 | centre tbl as one calculated grid | Terminal tbl centering now calculates its source-level offset once from the upstream `tbl_term.c` grid width: column fields and inter-column spacing count, layout-frame glyphs count only through the table's outer margins, and an overwide grid consumes its historical one-cell adjustment before integer centering. Rules and data rows use that same stored offset, so intersections cannot drift by a cell. Focused coverage plus `regress/tbl/opt/center` are ASCII byte-identical. Strict native Clippy passes and the deterministic twelve-way M9 replay advances to **646 exact / 13 different / 0 errors** across 659 artifacts. | active |
| 2026-08-27 | retain tbl ranges inside mdoc column-list items | A tbl range wrapped in an otherwise empty `Bl -column` item now remains structural in terminal projection: its direct `Table` children are rendered with the list field indentation, then the following column item owns its normal blank boundary. This prevents the column-cell text collector from discarding generated tbl rows while retaining the legacy AST wrapper and flat table rows. Focused renderer coverage plus `regress/tbl/macro/column` are ASCII byte-identical. Strict native Clippy passes and the deterministic twelve-way M9 replay advances to **647 exact / 12 different / 0 errors** across 659 artifacts. | active |
| 2026-08-27 | render bounded tbl text blocks | Terminal tbl rendering now preserves `T{…T}` as a private multi-line device field. It mirrors `tblcalc`'s per-column default width, lets explicit `w` override that field, measures wrapped lines before resolving grid width, and emits the matching blank continuation cells and borders. The public text-block marker and flattened canonical `TableCell` text are unchanged. Focused coverage plus `regress/tbl/data/{block_width,block_wrap}` are ASCII byte-identical. Strict native Clippy and `git diff --check` pass; the deterministic twelve-way M9 replay advances to **649 exact / 10 different / 0 errors** across 659 artifacts. | active |
| 2026-08-27 | reproduce tbl ASCII boundary intersections across plain, boxed, allbox, and doublebox grids | Private tbl layout now keeps standalone-leading-rule provenance, so a reserved outer column does not become a premature vertical stroke. The terminal grid extends horizontal layout cells through their owned half-gaps, resolves directional single/double intersections, and carries an internal double edge into the preceding/following `allbox` rule. `doublebox` emits its unadorned heavy outer rule before/after the layout-aware ordinary frame. This leaves the public `TableCell` contract untouched. Focused metadata coverage plus the complete `regress/tbl/layout/lines` ASCII corpus are byte-identical; the deterministic twelve-way M9 replay advances to **650 exact / 9 different / 0 errors** across 659 artifacts. | active |
| 2026-08-27 | retain tbl recovery columns and full-rule grid context | A raw tbl field recovered after trailing `S` layout cells now starts at the end of the public cell's occupied span; that coordinate remains private to the terminal row. This preserves list fields, blank boundaries, item numbering, and the source-positioned recovered closer without changing the compatible arena or canonical AST. Focused renderer coverage plus `regress/mdoc/Bl/broken` are ASCII byte-identical. The renderer derives that fact from immutable Body sibling/source-line provenance, without expanding the AST. Focused coverage plus `regress/mdoc/Bk/inputlines` are ASCII byte-identical. Within a `Bk` scope, function-signature commas retain the next argument with a device nonbreaking space while preserving the distinct `Fn`/`Fo` internal-argument behavior. The temporary C oracle remains deliberately feature-gated until M11 removal. | complete |
| 2026-08-27 | make M10 robustness evidence fast, deterministic, and independently scheduled | The routine native work-loop now builds once and uses a bounded 12-shard/20-worker differential wave, while stable tests replay all 8,132 checked-in parser fuzz seeds in four independent sessions, exercise renderer seeds in every format, stress eight concurrent parser sessions, and cover a valid 1 MiB input plus an exact root-byte boundary. Long mutation fuzzing and Miri are deliberately moved to the scheduled/manual `Mantdoc robustness` workflow so they do not inflate PR latency. A same-input generated parser benchmark records 146,561 / 1,348,200 / 18,147,644 ns for small/medium/large cases. The 35-page real-corpus replay exposed a true inline `\\\"` control-argument comment bug; scanner-stage stripping now keeps formatter annotations out of visible macro arguments, restoring clean results for `groff_man_style.7` and Fedora `sh.1`. The remaining content/layout candidates match their ledger's documented reference differences or intentional recursion-boundary reference failure; structure and projection are 35/35 clean. | active |

It was generated only after the complete C-backed L2 differential reached 572/572 exact, then immediately re-verified by a binary that has no legacy feature or C dependency. The manifest checker locks its provenance header, record count, aggregate hash, full-file hash, and record syntax; this gives M11 a durable native-only parser gate without making the roughly minute-long full replay part of routine developer feedback. | active |

The compact parallel M3–M6/M9 daily gate remains C-free and passed, while the full native snapshot remains release-only. The old crate directory and C-specific scripts are intentionally not silently deleted: the repository policy requires explicit approval for that irreversible 267-file removal. | active |
